Verification, Validation and Testing
480
Verification Process
482
Verification Process
481
Verification determined whether the system was built according to its specified requirements and design. It was carried out by reviewing the requirement tables against the implemented routes and models, by inspecting the role guards and the scoring function against their written specification, and by confirming that every module in the module register could be reached and exercised. Verification is a check on the build rather than on the users' judgement of it, which is why it is reported separately from validation.
483

It was carried out by reviewing the requirement tables against the implemented routes and models, by inspecting the role guards and the scoring function against their written specification, and by confirming that every module in the module register could be reached and exercised. Verification is a check on the build rather than on the users' judgement of it, which is why it is reported separately from validation.
482
Validation Process
484
Validation Process
483
Validation determined whether the system met the needs of its intended users. It was carried out with the IT experts, who examined all three role interfaces and rated the platform against the nine quality characteristics, and its results are reported in Chapter 3. Validation was not limited to whether a page loads: it asked whether an applicant can see why a vacancy does or does not fit, whether an employer can compare candidates on evidence rather than on impression, and whether PESO can follow a referral from application through probation.
485
Validation determined whether the system met the needs of its intended users. It was carried out with the IT experts, who examined all three role interfaces and rated the platform against the eight quality characteristics, and its results are reported in Chapter 3. Validation was not limited to whether a page loads: it asked whether an applicant can see why a vacancy does or does not fit, whether an employer can compare candidates on evidence rather than on impression, and whether PESO can follow a referral from application through probation.

Unit Testing
503
Unit testing is conducted at the module level to verify that each component of SkillUp performs its intended function independently. The research team uses structured test cases for each module: Account Registration, Verification, and Access; Applicant Profile and Skill Management; Job Vacancy and Skill Requirement; Skill-Based Matching and Skill Gap Analysis; Job Application and Hiring Pipeline; Training Recommendation, Enrollment, and Certification; PESO Coordination and Compliance; and Analytics and Reporting.

User Acceptance Testing (UAT)
545
User Acceptance Testing is the final phase of testing. It involves the IT experts, who act in the roles of job applicant, employer representative, and PESO administrator and interact with the system under realistic scenarios to validate that it meets the practical needs of each role. They assess the full set of ISO/IEC 25010:2023 software quality characteristics.

CHAPTER 3
547
RESULTS, DISCUSSION, AND INTERPRETATION OF DATA
548
This chapter presents the results of the study, explains how SkillUp performed, and interprets the data gathered during testing and evaluation. The results are presented in the order of the objectives stated in Chapter 1, so that each finding can be traced to the objective it addresses. The data come from two sources: the system testing, which verified that the platform’s functions work as specified, and the respondents’ evaluation, in which IT experts rated the system’s quality after examining it.
549
Test Environment and Participants
548
Test Environment and Participants
550
Testing and evaluation were conducted on the deployment described under Physical Specifications, over a web browser, with the demonstration database in place. The participants were five IT professionals from industry and academe who examined the three role interfaces, and their profile is reported in Chapter 2. The test environment is therefore the same environment in which the system would be demonstrated, which makes the results representative of that setting but not of a production deployment under load.
549
Testing and evaluation were conducted on the deployment described under Physical Specifications, over a web browser, with the demonstration database in place. The participants were five IT professionals from industry and academe who examined the three role interfaces, and their profile is reported in Chapter 2. The test environment is therefore the same environment in which the system would be demonstrated, which makes the results representative of that setting but not of a production deployment under load.
551
Defect Handling and Regression
550
Defect Handling and Regression
552
A case passed only when the observed behaviour matched the stated expected result; a partial result was recorded as a defect rather than as a pass. Defects were returned to the phase that caused them, so a requirement gap returned to requirements analysis, a structural fault to design, and a coding fault to implementation. The corrected build was then re-tested, and the tests that had already passed were re-run so that a fix did not break a working path.
551
A case passed only when the observed behaviour matched the stated expected result; a partial result was recorded as a defect rather than as a pass. Defects were returned to the phase that caused them, so a requirement gap returned to requirements analysis, a structural fault to design, and a coding fault to implementation. The corrected build was then re-tested, and the tests that had already passed were re-run so that a fix did not break a working path.

CHAPTER 3 — RESULTS, DISCUSSION, AND INTERPRETATION OF DATA
553
This chapter presents the results of the study, explains how SkillUp performed, and interprets the data gathered during testing and evaluation. The results are presented in the order of the objectives stated in Chapter 1, so that each finding can be traced to the objective it addresses. The data come from two sources: the system testing, which verified that the platform’s functions work as specified, and the respondents’ evaluation, in which IT experts rated the system’s quality after examining it.
553
System Testing Results
554
System Testing Results
554
Functional Testing
555
Functional Testing
555
Functional testing verified that the system does what the requirements specify. Unit testing exercised the sixteen modules individually: account access, résumé parsing and its fallback, skill inventory, the diagnostic assessment, vacancy management, matching and gap analysis, the interview pipeline, slot decrement, the course player, the capstone examination, certificate verification, and retention auditing. Integration testing then confirmed that the modules operate together: role guards hold, a parsed résumé populates the profile, a published vacancy enters the ranked search, a gap links to an aligned course, passing the capstone changes the provenance of the relevant skill, and closing a vacancy withdraws active applications. System testing exercised the complete platform against the nine quality characteristics. Every case in the three plans passed, as recorded in Tables 15 to 17.
556
Functional testing verified that the system does what the requirements specify. Unit testing exercised the sixteen modules individually: account access, résumé parsing and its fallback, skill inventory, the diagnostic assessment, vacancy management, matching and gap analysis, the interview pipeline, slot decrement, the course player, the capstone examination, certificate verification, and retention auditing. Integration testing then confirmed that the modules operate together: role guards hold, a parsed résumé populates the profile, a published vacancy enters the ranked search, a gap links to an aligned course, passing the capstone changes the provenance of the relevant skill, and closing a vacancy withdraws active applications. System testing exercised the complete platform against the eight quality characteristics. Every case in the three plans passed, as recorded in Tables 2.12 to 2.14.
556
Non-Functional Testing
557
Non-Functional Testing
557
Non-functional testing examined the properties that the functional cases cannot show. Determinism was tested by repeating an identical compatibility computation ten times and confirming that the score did not change. Access control was tested by attempting to reach each role's pages from another role, and every attempt was refused. Compatibility was tested by rendering the three portals in Chrome, Edge, Firefox, and Safari, where the layouts and charts were consistent. Performance was assessed structurally rather than by load test: at the evaluated volume of 62 applicants and 164 open vacancies, every portal responded within the demonstration threshold, and the applicant-by-vacancy cost of the employer dashboard was recorded as the scaling limit. Reliability under continuous load and recovery from an interrupted connection remain the least demonstrated properties, which is consistent with the Reliability rating reported in the evaluation.
558

Results and Discussion
660
Results and Discussion
672
The evaluation results are consistent with the literature reviewed in Chapter 1 and with the design decisions described in Chapter 2. Three findings merit discussion. First, Interaction Capability obtained the highest rating at 3.76. This is the expected outcome of a design in which every result is accompanied by its explanation: the score, the three component scores, and the per-skill breakdown are presented together rather than as a single figure, which is the condition that person-job recommendation research identifies as necessary for a recommendation to be usable (Tang et al., 2025). Second, Reliability obtained the lowest rating at 3.56, although still within the Excellent band. This is consistent with the deployment described in Chapter 2: SkillUp runs on a single host with a local database and no asynchronous worker, so behaviour under continuous load and recovery from interruption are the system's least demonstrated properties. The result is therefore a finding about the deployment rather than about the matching logic. Third, the narrow spread of the nine characteristic means, from 3.56 to 3.76, indicates that no single quality attribute was allowed to compensate for another, which is what the ISO/IEC 25010:2023 model is intended to detect.
661

Second, Flexibility obtained the lowest rating at 3.41, although still within the Strongly Agree band; Reliability was 3.54. The IT expert evaluation of SkillUp yielded a Grand Weighted Mean of 3.66, which is interpreted as Excellent based on the adopted four-point Likert scale. This result indicates that the system, as evaluated by qualified IT professionals across all three user role scenarios, fully and excellently satisfies the quality criteria assessed in this study.

All nine quality characteristics received an Excellent rating. The consistent Excellent ratings across the remaining characteristics further confirm that SkillUp meets its stated quality requirements completely and without critical deficiencies.

These observations represent targeted directions for future development rather than fundamental quality failures.

A dash in Table 3.11 indicates that the reviewed source does not report the feature; it does not indicate that the feature is absent from the system.
691
PESMATCH. PESMATCH established that a municipal employment office will adopt a matching tool and that a percentage is an intelligible output for its staff (Ycoy et al., 2022). It matches on preference and qualification as declared, however, and does not represent the requirements of a vacancy as normalised skills. SkillUp differs in treating the vacancy as a set of required skills drawn from the same registry the applicant uses, which is what makes a per-skill gap possible.
680
PESMATCH. PESMATCH established that a municipal employment office will adopt a matching tool and that a percentage is an intelligible output for its staff (Ycoy et al., 2022). It matches on preference and qualification as declared, however, and does not represent the requirements of a vacancy as normalised skills. SkillUp differs in treating the vacancy as a set of required skills drawn from the same registry the applicant uses, which is what makes a per-skill gap possible.
692
PhilJobNet and the PESO Employment Information System. The national platform supplies the regulatory frame within which SkillUp operates: it defines how vacancies are registered, how applicants are referred, and how placements are reported (Department of Labor and Employment, 2016). Its documented limitation is that vacancy skills are not expressed in a taxonomy shared with the training system, which is precisely the gap that SkillUp closes internally (Bayudan-Dacuycuy et al., 2024). SkillUp is therefore complementary to the national system rather than a replacement for it, and does not claim to transmit records to it.
681
PhilJobNet and the PESO Employment Information System. The national platform supplies the regulatory frame within which SkillUp operates: it defines how vacancies are registered, how applicants are referred, and how placements are reported (Department of Labor and Employment, 2016). Its documented limitation is that vacancy skills are not expressed in a taxonomy shared with the training system, which is precisely the gap that SkillUp closes internally (Bayudan-Dacuycuy et al., 2024). SkillUp is therefore complementary to the national system rather than a replacement for it, and does not claim to transmit records to it.
693
IT career course recommender. Hien et al. (2024) demonstrate that a rule-driven recommender can derive courses from a learner's identified gaps and career goals. The difference is the source of the requirements: SkillUp derives them from a live vacancy posted by a local employer, so the skill a person is trained for is the skill that a specific employer has asked for. That difference also carries a limitation, since a vacancy-derived gap is only as reliable as the employer's specification of the vacancy.
682
IT career course recommender. Hien et al. (2024) demonstrate that a rule-driven recommender can derive courses from a learner's identified gaps and career goals. The difference is the source of the requirements: SkillUp derives them from a live vacancy posted by a local employer, so the skill a person is trained for is the skill that a specific employer has asked for. That difference also carries a limitation, since a vacancy-derived gap is only as reliable as the employer's specification of the vacancy.
694
Industry upskilling tool. Braun et al. (2024a) describe a tool that identifies an individual's skill gaps and matches them to training modules within a Swedish upskilling programme. SkillUp shares the gap-to-training mechanism but wraps it in a hiring pipeline and a certification gate, so that completion changes the applicant's matching profile and produces a publicly verifiable credential.
683
Industry upskilling tool. Braun et al. (2024a) describe a tool that identifies an individual's skill gaps and matches them to training modules within a Swedish upskilling programme. SkillUp shares the gap-to-training mechanism but wraps it in a hiring pipeline and a certification gate, so that completion changes the applicant's matching profile and produces a publicly verifiable credential.
695
ESCO-based matching. Stalidis and Kyriazidou (2024) show that a standardised occupational taxonomy can be used to compare job roles with candidate skills, and also that such standards lag what employers request. SkillUp takes the same approach with a locally curated registry, which allows it to be maintained against local demand, at the cost of the external comparability that a European standard provides.
684
ESCO-based matching. Stalidis and Kyriazidou (2024) show that a standardised occupational taxonomy can be used to compare job roles with candidate skills, and also that such standards lag what employers request. SkillUp takes the same approach with a locally curated registry, which allows it to be maintained against local demand, at the cost of the external comparability that a European standard provides.
696
The comparison isolates what no reviewed system provides. PESMATCH and PhilJobNet operate the referral relationship but do not diagnose skills. The two recommenders diagnose gaps but draw their requirements from learner goals or from an upskilling programme rather than from live vacancies in an employment service. ESCO supplies a taxonomy but no workflow. Only SkillUp joins the six functions on one record: a vacancy with normalised required skills, an applicant with provenance-tagged skills, a deterministic and decomposable gap analysis over the two, a training recommendation derived from that gap, a certification that writes the trained skill back into the applicant's profile, and a placement whose probation and retention outcome is recorded against the same referral, all under the oversight of a public employment office. That join, rather than any single feature, is the contribution of the study, and it is the integration gap identified in Chapter 1.
685

Hypothesis Testing Results
686
Hypothesis Testing Results
698
This section tests the hypothesis stated in Chapter 1. The null hypothesis is that the SkillUp platform does not have a significant effect on system quality and on the efficiency of the employment process; the alternative is that it does. Because the study measured quality through expert ratings rather than through placement outcomes, the hypothesis is tested on those ratings: whether the mean rating on each ISO/IEC 25010:2023 characteristic is significantly greater than 2.50, the midpoint of the four-point scale and the boundary between Fair and Good.
687
The hypothesis was tested as a benchmark against the scale midpoint. The null hypothesis states that the expert ratings for the eight ISO/IEC 25010:2023 characteristics are not significantly greater than 2.50; the alternative states that they are. This is a test of whether the ratings clear the midpoint, not a test of the platform’s effect on employment outcomes.
699
A one-sample t-test was used at the .05 level of significance. The evaluator, not the individual rating, was treated as the unit of analysis: each evaluator's mean across the ratings for a characteristic formed one observation, giving five observations per characteristic and five for the overall rating, with four degrees of freedom. Treating the five evaluators as independent units avoids the inflation that would follow from counting every rating as a separate case. Cohen's d is reported as an indication of effect size. Table 30 reports the results.
688
A one-sample, one-tailed t-test was used at the .05 level. Each evaluator’s mean for a characteristic formed one observation, giving five observations and four degrees of freedom per characteristic. This avoids treating every item rating as an independent case. Table 3.12 reports the results.

Every characteristic and the overall rating fall significantly above the midpoint. The overall mean was 3.66 (SD = 0.217), t(4) = 11.93, p < .001, d = 5.34. The smallest test statistic was recorded for Performance Efficiency, t(4) = 8.70, p < .001, and the largest for Security, t(4) = 11.57, p < .001. The null hypothesis is therefore rejected at the .05 level and the alternative hypothesis is supported: the evaluators rated the platform significantly above the midpoint of the scale on all eight quality characteristics.
700
All eight characteristics and the overall rating fall above the midpoint. The overall mean was 3.605 (SD = 0.245), t(4) = 10.081, p < .001. The smallest test statistic was Flexibility, t(4) = 6.635, p < .01; the largest was Interaction Capability, t(4) = 12.419, p < .001. The null hypothesis is therefore rejected at the .05 level, and the alternative is supported: the evaluators rated every characteristic significantly above the midpoint.
712
Two limits qualify this result. The panel was purposive and small, so the test describes these five evaluators rather than a population of IT professionals, and the large test statistics partly reflect how closely the ratings agree. More importantly, the ratings measure judged software quality. The study did not observe placements, hiring decisions, or time-to-fill, so the clause of the alternative hypothesis on the efficiency of the employment process is not tested here; it rests on the functional evidence under System Testing Results. The result should be read as evidence that the platform was judged to meet the quality standard, not as a causal estimate of its effect on employment.
701
Two limits qualify the result. First, the panel is purposive and small, so the test describes these five evaluators rather than a population of IT professionals; the large statistics partly reflect how closely the ratings agree. Second, the test measures judged software quality, not placement, hiring, or time-to-fill. The employment-process claim is therefore outside this test and rests only on the functional evidence in Chapter 3. The applicant TAM results are reported descriptively and are not part of this hypothesis test.

CHAPTER 4
702
CHAPTER 4 — CONCLUSION AND RECOMMENDATIONS
714
CONCLUSION AND RECOMMENDATIONS
715
This chapter synthesises the study. It restates what was undertaken, summarises the findings reported in Chapter 3, draws conclusions anchored on those findings, and sets out recommendations for the stakeholders of the platform and for future researchers.
703
This chapter synthesises the study. It restates what was undertaken, summarises the findings reported in Chapter 3, draws conclusions anchored on those findings, and sets out recommendations for the stakeholders of the platform and for future researchers.
716
Summary of the Study
704
Summary of the Study
717
This study designed, developed, and evaluated SkillUp, a web-based employment facilitation platform that connects job applicants, employers, and a Public Employment Service Office around a single record of skills, vacancies, and placements. It set out to determine how structured skills data, automated gap analysis, native interactive training, and municipal oversight could be combined to connect applicants with vacancies and to address workforce skill shortages in Bocaue, Bulacan.
705
This study designed, developed, and evaluated SkillUp, a web-based employment facilitation platform that connects job applicants, employers, and a Public Employment Service Office around a single record of skills, vacancies, and placements. It set out to determine how structured skills data, automated gap analysis, native interactive training, and municipal oversight could be combined to connect applicants with vacancies and to address workforce skill shortages in Bocaue, Bulacan.
718
The study used a quantitative, descriptive design. Five IT experts served as respondents and evaluated the system against the nine quality characteristics of the ISO/IEC 25010:2023 product quality model, using a questionnaire that had first been content-validated by five IT professionals. Development followed the Iterative Waterfall model, and the resulting platform comprises 22 data models, 92 routes, 50 role-partitioned templates, and 16 functional modules, including a skills registry, a deterministic matching engine, a three-stage hiring pipeline, a pre-employment compliance checklist, a learning management system with a capstone assessment and a publicly verifiable certificate, and a labour-market analytics module.
706
The study used a quantitative, descriptive design. Five IT experts served as respondents and evaluated the system against the eight quality characteristics of the ISO/IEC 25010:2023 product quality model, using a questionnaire that had first been content-validated by five IT professionals. Development followed the Iterative Waterfall model, and the resulting platform comprises 22 data models, 92 routes, 50 role-partitioned templates, and 16 functional modules, including a skills registry, a deterministic matching engine, a three-stage hiring pipeline, a pre-employment compliance checklist, a learning management system with a capstone assessment and a publicly verifiable certificate, and a labour-market analytics module.
719
Summary of Findings
707
Summary of Findings
720
The system was completed and verified. Unit testing covered account access, résumé parsing and its fallback, skill inventory, the diagnostic assessment, vacancy management, matching and gap analysis, the interview pipeline, slot decrement, the course player, the capstone examination, certificate verification, and retention auditing, and all listed test cases passed. Integration testing confirmed that role guards hold, that a parsed résumé populates the profile, that a published vacancy enters the ranked search, that a gap links to an aligned course, that passing the capstone changes the provenance of the relevant skill, and that closing a vacancy withdraws active applications.
708
The system was completed and verified. Unit testing covered account access, résumé parsing and its fallback, skill inventory, the diagnostic assessment, vacancy management, matching and gap analysis, the interview pipeline, slot decrement, the course player, the capstone examination, certificate verification, and retention auditing, and all listed test cases passed. Integration testing confirmed that role guards hold, that a parsed résumé populates the profile, that a published vacancy enters the ranked search, that a gap links to an aligned course, that passing the capstone changes the provenance of the relevant skill, and that closing a vacancy withdraws active applications.
721
The instrument itself was validated before use. Across 825 ratings from five IT professionals, the instrument obtained an overall mean of 3.70, and every component, including all nine characteristics, was rated Highly Appropriate. No item-level rating fell below 3.
709
The evaluation of the system produced a Grand Weighted Mean of 3.59, interpreted as Strongly Agree. All eight quality characteristics fell within the Strongly Agree range. Interaction Capability obtained the highest mean at 3.72, followed by Maintainability at 3.65. Flexibility obtained the lowest mean at 3.41; Reliability was 3.54.
722
The evaluation of the system produced a Grand Weighted Mean of 3.66, interpreted as Excellent. All nine quality characteristics fell within the Excellent range. Interaction Capability and Maintainability obtained the highest means at 3.76 each, Security and Safety obtained 3.68 each, Functional Suitability and Compatibility obtained 3.64 each, Performance Efficiency and Flexibility obtained 3.60 each, and Reliability obtained the lowest mean at 3.56.
723
The descriptive results bear on the hypotheses stated in Chapter 1. Every characteristic mean lies in the upper quarter of the four-point scale and no item mean falls below 3.20, which is consistent with the alternative hypothesis that SkillUp produces a favourable effect on system quality, skills-based matching, gap identification and training recommendation. This study therefore reports the direction and size of that effect rather than a tested probability.
710
The descriptive results bear on the hypotheses stated in Chapter 1. Every characteristic mean lies in the upper quarter of the four-point scale and no item mean falls below 3.20, which is consistent with the alternative hypothesis that SkillUp produces a favourable effect on system quality, skills-based matching, gap identification and training recommendation. This study therefore reports the direction and size of that effect rather than a tested probability.
724
Conclusion
711
Conclusion
725
The study set out to determine whether an integrated platform could use structured skills data to connect applicants with vacancies, identify gaps, and direct applicants to training, and the findings support the conclusion that it can. Every specific objective stated in Chapter 1 was met: the centralised taxonomy was built with provenance-tagged evidence, employers were able to publish vacancies with required skills drawn from that taxonomy, the matching engine produced deterministic and decomposable scores, missing skills were mapped to training, completion wrote the trained skill back into the applicant's profile, and PESO was given verification, compliance, retention, and analytics functions.
712
The study set out to determine whether an integrated platform could use structured skills data to connect applicants with vacancies, identify gaps, and direct applicants to training, and the findings support the conclusion that it can. Every specific objective stated in Chapter 1 was met: the centralised taxonomy was built with provenance-tagged evidence, employers were able to publish vacancies with required skills drawn from that taxonomy, the matching engine produced deterministic and decomposable scores, missing skills were mapped to training, completion wrote the trained skill back into the applicant's profile, and PESO was given verification, compliance, retention, and analytics functions.
726
The evaluation supports the conclusion that SkillUp is a high-quality system by the standard adopted. A Grand Weighted Mean of 3.66, with every characteristic rated Excellent, indicates that the platform satisfies its functional and non-functional requirements without a critical deficiency. The result also supports the narrower conclusion that a deterministic, explainable scoring rule can satisfy evaluators, which is consistent with the literature's concern for explainability over expressive power in hiring systems.
713
The evaluation supports the conclusion that SkillUp is a high-quality system by the standard adopted. A Grand Weighted Mean of 3.59, with every characteristic rated Strongly Agree, indicates that the platform satisfies its functional and non-functional requirements without a critical deficiency. The applicant TAM results were also above the midpoint, but they are reported descriptively and are not part of the hypothesis test. The result also supports the narrower conclusion that a deterministic, explainable scoring rule can satisfy evaluators, which is consistent with the literature's concern for explainability over expressive power in hiring systems.
727
Two conclusions must be stated with their limits. First, the high rating of the matching function concerns its completeness, correctness, and usefulness as judged by IT experts; it does not establish that the weights produce good hiring outcomes, because no placement outcome was used to calibrate them. Second, Reliability obtained the lowest rating, and that result concerns the deployment environment as much as the software: a single host, a local database, and the absence of an asynchronous worker are properties of the evaluated configuration and should not be read as properties of the matching logic.
714
Two conclusions must be stated with their limits. First, the high rating of the matching function concerns its completeness, correctness, and usefulness as judged by IT experts; it does not establish that the weights produce good hiring outcomes, because no placement outcome was used to calibrate them. Second, Reliability obtained the lowest rating, and that result concerns the deployment environment as much as the software: a single host, a local database, and the absence of an asynchronous worker are properties of the evaluated configuration and should not be read as properties of the matching logic.
728
Finally, the study concludes that the integration itself is the contribution. The value of SkillUp to a PESO does not lie in any single module, since matching tools, job portals, and learning platforms each exist separately, but in the fact that one record carries an applicant from a skills claim through a vacancy, a gap diagnosis, a training course, a certificate, and a placement whose retention can be reported.
715
Finally, the study concludes that the integration itself is the contribution. The value of SkillUp to a PESO does not lie in any single module, since matching tools, job portals, and learning platforms each exist separately, but in the fact that one record carries an applicant from a skills claim through a vacancy, a gap diagnosis, a training course, a certificate, and a placement whose retention can be reported.
729
Recommendations
716
Recommendations
730
For the partner PESO. The platform should be adopted first as a monitoring and referral instrument rather than as a replacement for existing practice. Staff should be trained on the referral workflow and on the interpretation of the composite score, and the office should enter enough placement outcomes to make the retention reports meaningful.
717
For the partner PESO. The platform should be adopted first as a monitoring and referral instrument rather than as a replacement for existing practice. Staff should be trained on the referral workflow and on the interpretation of the composite score, and the office should enter enough placement outcomes to make the retention reports meaningful.
731
For employers. Employers should specify vacancy requirements carefully, because the quality of a gap analysis cannot exceed the quality of the requirement it is computed from. Employers should also record probation and retention outcomes, since those records are what make the office's placement figures defensible.
718
For employers. Employers should specify vacancy requirements carefully, because the quality of a gap analysis cannot exceed the quality of the requirement it is computed from. Employers should also record probation and retention outcomes, since those records are what make the office's placement figures defensible.
732
For applicants. Applicants should treat the gap report as a plan rather than a verdict, and should use the training catalogue to close the specific gaps a target vacancy identifies. They should also review every field extracted from an uploaded résumé before relying on it.
719
For applicants. Applicants should treat the gap report as a plan rather than a verdict, and should use the training catalogue to close the specific gaps a target vacancy identifies. They should also review every field extracted from an uploaded résumé before relying on it.
733
For the institution. The college may use the platform as a demonstration case for capstone work that integrates development with a recognised quality model, and may retain the validation and evaluation instruments as templates for subsequent cohorts.
720
For the institution. The college may use the platform as a demonstration case for capstone work that integrates development with a recognised quality model, and may retain the validation and evaluation instruments as templates for subsequent cohorts.
734
For future researchers. The most useful next step is a longitudinal study that follows a cohort from enrolment through training, placement, and retention, so that the matching weights can be calibrated against actual outcomes instead of set by hand. A second step is a fairness audit that measures outcomes across applicant groups, since the present study excluded protected attributes from the score but did not measure the score's effects.
721
For future researchers. The most useful next step is a longitudinal study that follows a cohort from enrolment through training, placement, and retention, so that the matching weights can be calibrated against actual outcomes instead of set by hand. A second step is a fairness audit that measures outcomes across applicant groups, since the present study excluded protected attributes from the score but did not measure the score's effects.
735
Future Work and Direction for Future Research
722
Future Work and Direction for Future Research
736
Several extensions follow directly from the limitations reported in Chapter 2. The screening rubric should become vacancy-specific, since it is currently fixed to front-end development criteria. Employer accreditation should become a distinct state with evidence and an expiry, instead of sharing one flag with email verification. Match scores should be precomputed and cached, because the employer dashboard currently recomputes every applicant against every open vacancy on each request.
723
Several extensions follow directly from the limitations reported in Chapter 2. The screening rubric should become vacancy-specific, since it is currently fixed to front-end development criteria. Employer accreditation should become a distinct state with evidence and an expiry, instead of sharing one flag with email verification. Match scores should be precomputed and cached, because the employer dashboard currently recomputes every applicant against every open vacancy on each request.
737
On the infrastructure side, the platform should move to a managed relational database, place AI-assisted work behind a task queue, and be served over TLS with an off-host backup before it holds real identification or medical documents. These are conditions of deployment rather than features of the system.
724
On the infrastructure side, the platform should move to a managed relational database, place AI-assisted work behind a task queue, and be served over TLS with an off-host backup before it holds real identification or medical documents. These are conditions of deployment rather than features of the system.
738
Two research questions remain unanswered and are worth pursuing. The first is whether gap-driven training recommendation actually raises placement rates relative to untargeted enrolment; answering it requires a comparison group and a longer observation window than this study had. The second is whether a provenance-weighted score, which distinguishes an assessed skill from a self-reported one, predicts placement better than the present binary treatment of skill presence. Both questions require data that a single municipal pilot cannot supply on its own.
725
Two research questions remain unanswered and are worth pursuing. The first is whether gap-driven training recommendation actually raises placement rates relative to untargeted enrolment; answering it requires a comparison group and a longer observation window than this study had. The second is whether a provenance-weighted score, which distinguishes an assessed skill from a self-reported one, predicts placement better than the present binary treatment of skill presence. Both questions require data that a single municipal pilot cannot supply on its own.
